    99	    # stderr; sys.exit(1). First mismatch wins — nothing catches SystemExit.

   106

   158	"""E2E item 35 — dogfood Level A (SPEC §18.3 item 35, §18.5): seed --demo, then run
   159	scripts/dogfood_cli.py as a subprocess against the live test server."""
   160	from __future__ import annotations
   161	
   162	import os
   163	import subprocess
   164	from pathlib import Path
   165	
   166	REPO_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]
   167	PYTHON = REPO_ROOT / ".venv" / "bin" / "python"      # same pinning style as conftest.PLAN_BIN
   168	SCRIPT = REPO_ROOT / "scripts" / "dogfood_cli.py"
   169	
   170	
   171	def test_e35_dogfood_level_a(server, cli):
   172	    cli(server, "seed", "--demo")                    # harness seeds; the script never does
   173	    env = {k: v for k, v in os.environ.items() if not k.startswith("PLAN_")}
   174	    env["PLAN_SERVER_URL"] = server.base
   175	    env["PLAN_DB_PATH"] = str(server.db_path)
   176	    proc = subprocess.run(

   211

   269	`days/api.py:52-79`, recon D):
   270	`day add-ticket {w.ticket_id}` → response is the day view; then `day show` → assert
   271	`w.ticket_id in [t["id"] for t in data["tickets"]]`.
   272	`day remove-ticket {w.ticket_id}` → then `day show` → assert `w.ticket_id not in
   273	[t["id"] ...]`; then `ticket show` (env `PLAN_TICKET_ID`) → assert
   274	`state == "in_progress"` (removal is deferral only, SPEC §3.4).
   275	Membership-only assertions: under a real clock (standalone run after 05:00) the demo
   276	day already holds 3 tickets, so counts/positions are never asserted.
   277	
   278	**Step 9 — blocking round-trip (SPEC §3.6, §7.2, item 9).**
   279	(a) Baseline: `pickup_ids()` via CLI `queue pickup` → assert `w.ticket_id` present.
   280	(b) CLI: `ticket create --title "Dogfood blocker ticket."` (defaults: P3, no deadline)
   281	→ `w.blocker_id`. (c) CLI: `link add {w.blocker_id} {w.ticket_id} --kind blocks` —
   282	from = blocker, to = blocked, confirmed against demo's `t8 -blocks-> t5`
   283	(`seed/demo.py:111`) and `core/links.py:134-156`. Assert response
   284	`{"from_id": w.blocker_id, "to_id": w.ticket_id, "kind": "blocks"}`.
   285	(d) Assert `w.ticket_id` ABSENT from `pickup_ids()`; secondary exact check on our
   286	entity: `GET /api/tickets/{w.ticket_id}` (detail view) → `blocked is True`
   287	(`tickets/views.py:144-147`). (e) Complete the blocker — HTTP-human:
   288	`POST /api/tickets/{w.blocker_id}/state` body `{"to": "done"}` (human state jump,
   289	`resolution.py:202-211`) → assert response `state == "done"`. (f) Assert `w.ticket_id`
   290	PRESENT in `pickup_ids()` again.
   291	
   292	**Step 10 — dispatcher claim (SPEC §7.1-7.3, §9 test mode).** HTTP:
   293	`POST /api/test/tick-dispatcher` (no body; test-endpoint, no auth distinction).
   294	Report per `runtime.py:126-218`. Budget 2 − 0 active = 2; eligible order puts our P0
   295	deadline-today ticket at/next-to the top (see baseline analysis above), so it is
   296	always claimed; the other slot goes to a demo ticket — ignored.
   297	Assert: `spawned` contains an entry `e` with `e["ticket_id"] == w.ticket_id` —
   298	membership, never `len(spawned)` — and save `w.run_id = e["run_id"]` (the
   299	HTTP-visible source, recon B). Then `GET /api/tickets/{w.ticket_id}` → assert
   300	`claim_active is True` (`views.py:59`). Then `w.claim =
   301	read_claim_lock(w.ticket_id)` from SQLite (read-only; the one sanctioned use,
   302	recon B) → assert non-empty.
   303	
   304	**Step 11 — claim-carrying worker writes (SPEC §7.3, §7.6, §8; recon E.11).** All CLI
   305	with env `PLAN_TICKET_ID=w.ticket_id`, `PLAN_RUN_ID=w.run_id`, `PLAN_CLAIM=w.claim`
   306	(the CLI turns these into `X-Plan-Run-Id`/`X-Plan-Claim` headers, `http.py:36-42`;
   307	`require_claim` validates token + running-run match, `authctx.py:114-181`).
   308	**Decision: heartbeat AND close are both exercised** (rationale in §4, D2). Order:
   309	1. `run heartbeat` → assert `data["run"]["id"] == w.run_id` and
   310	   `isinstance(data["claim_expires"], int)` (`dispatch/api.py:30-39`).
   311	2. `propose result --body-file -`, stdin `RESULT_BODY` → auto-accepts
   312	   (`in_progress` target `needs_review` ≤ ceiling `needs_review`). Assert:
   313	   `state == "needs_review"`, `fields.result.value == RESULT_BODY`,
   314	   `fields.result.proposal is None`, and — auto-accepts change neither grant half
   315	   (SPEC §4.4.7, item 36) — `ceiling == "needs_review"`, `at_cap == "propose"`.
   316	3. `run close --outcome done --summary -`, stdin `RUN_SUMMARY` → assert
   317	   `data["run"]["status"] == "done"`, `data["run"]["summary"] == RUN_SUMMARY`
   318	   (`dispatch/api.py:42-57`). Then `GET /api/tickets/{w.ticket_id}` → assert
   319	   `claim_active is False` (lease cleared by `_finalize_run`, `dispatch/data.py:149-153`).
   320	
   321	**Step 12 — human approve (SPEC §4.4.5).** HTTP-human:
   322	`POST /api/tickets/{w.ticket_id}/approve` (empty JSON body `{}`; route takes none).
   323	Assert response `state == "done"`. Print the final summary line, exit 0.
   324	
   325	Every step ends with one `PASS step NN — <name>` line; the first mismatch prints
   326	`FAIL step NN — <name>` + expected/got (reprs) to stderr and exits 1.

   329	
   330	## 3. The four skills documents (SPEC §17; each ≤ 150 lines)
   331	
   332	Binding grounding rule for the implementer: **every CLI example is first run against a
   333	live demo-seeded test server and transcribed from real output** — boot
   334	`PLAN_TEST_MODE=1 PLAN_DB_PATH=<tmp> PLAN_PORT=<free> .venv/bin/plan serve`, run
   335	`PLAN_SERVER_URL=… .venv/bin/plan seed --demo`, then execute each documented command
   336	and copy the real flags and JSON field names. No invented flags, no invented fields.
   337	The verb surface is exactly `src/planner/cli/main.py`; anything not there (accept,
   338	approve, grant, unblock, day-plan decisions) does not exist in the CLI and must not
   339	appear as a command in any skill. Tone: imperative, written FOR the agent loading it,
   340	concrete commands, no meta-commentary about the spec or the build.
   341	
   342	### 3.1 `skills/planning-worker.md` — the dispatched worker loop (~100 lines)
   343	
   344	1. **You and your env** — you were spawned by the dispatcher to work one ticket.
   345	   `PLAN_SERVER_URL` (the server), `PLAN_TICKET_ID` (your ticket — every verb defaults
   346	   to it), `PLAN_RUN_ID` + `PLAN_CLAIM` (your lease: the CLI attaches them to every
   347	   write; a write without them, or after the lease lapses, is rejected with
   348	   `stale_claim`). Never unset or share them.
   349	2. **Orient first** — `plan ticket show --json`; read `state`, `ceiling`, `at_cap`,
   350	   the four `fields` slots (`value` = decided, `proposal` = pending, `notes` =
   351	   guidance to honor), `recap`. The gating field per state (success → approach → plan
   352	   → result table).
   353	3. **The write model: proposals only** — you never write values or states. File the
   354	   current gating field; below the ceiling it auto-accepts and the ticket advances; at
   355	   the ceiling with `at_cap=propose` it parks for the human; a new proposal on the
   356	   same field replaces the pending one. Example (transcribed):
   357	   `plan propose approach --body-file -` with a heredoc body; long text always via
   358	   stdin or `--body-file`, never inline.
   359	4. **Recap discipline** — after every meaningful unit of work overwrite the recap:
   360	   `plan recap --body-file -`. It is the ticket's running memory; rejected only while
   361	   the ticket still sits at `needs_success`.
   362	5. **Notes** — `plan note <field> --body-file -` for guidance you want kept next to a
   363	   field (e.g. review notes on `result`).
   364	6. **Heartbeat cadence** — `plan run heartbeat` at least every 10 minutes (lease TTL
   365	   15 minutes); a lapsed lease means every further write fails and the run is
   366	   reclaimed.
   367	7. **Finishing** — work to the ceiling, then `plan run close --outcome done --summary -`
   368	   (stdin summary). Stuck on something you cannot resolve: write what you know into
   369	   the recap and notes, then `plan run close --outcome blocked --summary -`.
   370	8. **Never ask questions** — no human is in this loop. Decide, propose, record in the
   371	   recap, close. Exit codes: 0 ok, 1 rejected write (read the JSON error on stderr),
   372	   2 server unreachable.
   373	
   374	### 3.2 `skills/planning-boundary.md` — the judgment-pass contract (~80 lines)
   375	
   376	Grounded in `core/adapters/real.py:109-150` and `core/adapters/base.py:37-56` (this
   377	agent is invoked non-interactively; it does not use the CLI).
   378	
   379	1. **When you run** — once per planning date at the morning boundary; you receive one
   380	   prompt and must reply with exactly one JSON object, nothing else around it.
   381	2. **Your inputs** (deterministic, DB-internal; given as JSON in the prompt):
   382	   `planning_date` (ISO); `carryover` — yesterday's unfinished day tickets as
   383	   `{id, title, state, priority}` digests; `overdue` — same digest shape, tickets and
   384	   items past deadline; `approvals_digest` — `{entity_id, kind, waiting_since}`
   385	   entries awaiting the human.
   386	3. **Judgment reply shape** (verbatim JSON skeleton):
   387	   `{"brief_markdown": "...", "plan_tree": {"root": {"focus": "...", "status":
   388	   "proposed"}, "children": [{"ticket_id": "t_…"|null, "note": "...", "status":
   389	   "proposed", "position": 0}]}}` — statuses always `"proposed"` (the system stores
   390	   your tree as a proposal for the human); `ticket_id` only from ids present in the
   391	   inputs, `null` for a note-only child; positions contiguous from 0; few children —
   392	   a focused day, not a backlog dump.
   393	4. **Brief guidance** — short markdown: the day's single focus first, then carryover
   394	   worth naming, overdue risks, and how much is waiting in approvals. No filler.
   395	5. **Replan-root call** — reply `{"plan_tree": {...}}` (same tree shape), a complete
   396	   replacement for the day.
   397	6. **Replan-child call** — you get the invalidated child + the same inputs; reply
   398	   `{"child": {"ticket_id": "t_…"|null, "note": "..."}}` — one replacement node only.
   399	7. **Budget** — 60 seconds; reply directly, no exploration, no questions, no text
   400	   outside the JSON object.
   401	
   402	### 3.3 `skills/planner-main.md` — the day/system chat agent (~90 lines)
   403	
   404	1. **You** — the planner in the day chat. You read and file through the CLI
   405	   (`PLAN_SERVER_URL` set; you carry no claim); all decisions (accepts, grants,
   406	   approvals, day-plan calls) belong to the human in the UI — you surface, summarize,
   407	   and capture, you never resolve.
   408	2. **Quick capture — the prime directive** — anything the human tosses into chat that
   409	   is work gets filed IMMEDIATELY as a ticket, anything that is an idea as an idea;
   410	   default P3; capture first, keep talking after:
   411	   `plan ticket create --title "…"` (add `--project`/`--deadline` only when stated);
   412	   `plan idea create --title "…" --body-file -` for bodies. Never ask "should I file
   413	   this?" — file it and say so.
   414	3. **Operating the queues** — `plan queue approvals --json` (what waits on the human,
   415	   oldest first), `plan queue pickup --json` (what the dispatcher can take next, in
   416	   order), `plan queue overdue --json` (past-deadline tickets/items). Transcribed
   417	   one-line output examples for each.
   418	4. **The day** — `plan day show --json` (brief, plan tree, ordered ticket list);
   419	   `plan day add-ticket <t_id>` / `plan day remove-ticket <t_id>` (today by default;
   420	   removing defers — ticket state untouched); `plan ticket set <t_id> --day today`.
   421	5. **Looking things up** — `plan ticket show <t_id> --json`, `plan ticket list
   422	   --state …`, `plan item list`, `plan sprint show --json`, `plan idea list`.
   423	6. **What you must route to the human** — accepting proposals, grants
   424	   (ceiling/at_cap), needs_review approval, unblocking, dropping, day-plan
   425	   accept/invalidate: name the thing, point to the Review screen or Ticket page.
   426	
   427	### 3.4 `skills/planning-executor.md` — owned in_progress work + the needs_review discipline (~90 lines)
   428	
   429	1. **Scope** — you own one `in_progress` ticket (dispatched with claim env, or handed
   430	   `PLAN_TICKET_ID`). Deliver the result; then drive the review discipline.
   431	2. **Work loop** — orient (`plan ticket show --json`): execute against
   432	   `fields.plan.value` and honor every `notes` slot; keep the recap current
   433	   (`plan recap --body-file -`) as you go; heartbeat if you carry a claim.
   434	3. **Landing the result** — `plan propose result --body-file -`: a result that states
   435	   what was produced, where it lives, and how it was verified. Below/at ceiling
   436	   `needs_review` it auto-accepts and the ticket moves to `needs_review`; re-proposing
   437	   replaces a pending result.
   438	4. **The review notes are the checklist** — `fields.result.notes` holds what needs
   439	   careful checking (written during planning, updated by you). Read them before
   440	   review; they are the review's contract (SPEC §4.2).
   441	5. **needs_review: orchestrate the Codex CLI** — run it non-interactively against the
   442	   result and the notes, e.g.
   443	   `codex exec "Review the following result against these review notes; list concrete
   444	   violations only: <result value + notes + the artifacts they point to>"`.
   445	   Feed it the real content (from `plan ticket show --json`), not a summary.
   446	6. **Fix–update loop** — fix everything codex surfaces; update the notes via
   447	   `plan note result --body-file -` recording what was checked, what was fixed, and
   448	   what a human should still eyeball; rerun codex until it reports no violations.
   449	7. **Hand-off** — you never approve. When codex is clean and the notes say so, the
   450	   recap states the ticket is ready; the human approves from Review. If review
   451	   surfaces something you cannot fix, write it into the notes and recap and (if
   452	   claimed) `plan run close --outcome blocked --summary -`.
   453	
   454	---
   455	
   456	## 4. Risks and open points — each resolved
   457	
   458	**R1 — demo data coexistence / real-vs-fake clock skew.** The demo seed writes with the
   459	real clock (`demo.py:29-30`) while the e2e server clock is fake (2026-07-04). Resolved
   460	by construction: every assertion is membership/absence of OUR entities; day verbs use
   461	default dates (server-resolved planning date); the walkthrough deadline uses the
   462	client-side real date so ordering beats/ties t4 under both clocks. In a tie
   463	(standalone run where demo real-today+1 equals script real-today — clock-midnight
   464	edge), t4 sorts first on `created_at` but both fit the 2-slot budget, so the step-10
   465	membership assertion is unaffected. No global counts anywhere.
   466	
   467	**R2 — recon C wording "the second slot will claim the demo P0".** True under the e2e
   468	fake clock; under a standalone real-clock run our ticket may take the second slot
   469	instead of the first. Not a contradiction — the plan never asserts which slot, only
   470	membership of `w.ticket_id` in `spawned`.
   471	
   472	**D1 — grant pairs per gate** (recon E.4/6/7): success accept `("none", "propose")`;
   473	approach edit-accept `("none", "propose")`; plan accept `("needs_review", "propose")`.
   474	The two `"none"` grants pin the ticket at each new gate with `propose`, forcing the
   475	next proposal to park for a human accept at every gate (item 35's requirement); the
   476	final grant makes the ticket eligible and routes the result into `needs_review`.
   477	
   478	**D2 — heartbeat and close: INCLUDED** (recon E.11 decision point). `close_run` only
   479	clears the lease and updates the breaker/run row (`dispatch/data.py:113-217`); it never
   480	touches ticket state, so `needs_review` → approve is undisturbed. Order matters only in
   481	that both claim verbs must run while the lease is live: heartbeat → propose result →
   482	close → (human) approve. This grounds `plan run heartbeat` and `plan run close` in the
   483	worker/executor skills with transcribed output.
   484	
   485	**D3 — claim token acquisition.** `run_id` from the tick report's `spawned` entry
   486	(HTTP-visible, preferred per recon B); the token from a read-only SQLite open of
   487	`PLAN_DB_PATH` (`file:…?mode=ro`), documented in the module docstring as harness
   488	scaffolding for the env block the real spawn passes (`real.py:52-56`). No API change,
   489	no other DB reads or any writes.
   490	
   491	**D4 — blocker completion path.** Human state jump `POST /api/tickets/{id}/state
   492	{"to": "done"}` (recon E.9): valid from `needs_success` (`resolution.py:202-211` allows
   493	any different non-dropped target; skipped gating values stay null, SPEC §4.3).
   494	
   495	**D5 — script HTTP client.** `httpx` (already a pinned dependency; the script always
   496	runs under `.venv` python — both in the test and the standalone gate). Human calls pass
   497	no custom headers at all, matching conftest's `api.human_post` proof that header-less
   498	POSTs pass `reject_agents`.
   499	
   500	**D6 — test subprocess interpreter.** Pinned `.venv/bin/python` by absolute path
   501	(conftest's `PLAN_BIN` pattern) rather than `sys.executable` — identical under
   502	`./verify`, unambiguous if a system pytest ever collects the suite.
   503	
   504	**D7 — one-anchor discipline.** Only `test_e35_dogfood_level_a` exists in the new test
   505	file; helpers (none needed) would carry non-`test_` names. T19/T20 own e28–e34 in
   506	sibling files; this file imports nothing from them.
   507	
   508	**Gates before hand-back (recon H):**
   509	1. `.venv/bin/pytest tests/e2e/test_dogfood.py -q` green.
   510	2. Standalone: `PLAN_TEST_MODE=1 PLAN_DB_PATH=$T/dog.db PLAN_PORT=<free>
   511	   PLAN_LOGS_DIR=$T/logs PLAN_DISPATCHER_LOCK_PATH=$T/dispatcher.lock
   512	   .venv/bin/plan serve` &; `PLAN_SERVER_URL=http://127.0.0.1:<port> .venv/bin/plan
   513	   seed --demo`; `PLAN_SERVER_URL=… PLAN_DB_PATH=$T/dog.db .venv/bin/python
   514	   scripts/dogfood_cli.py` → exit 0, full 12-line PASS trace. (`PLAN_FAKE_NOW`
   515	   optional — the script is clock-agnostic by design, R1.)
   516	3. `.venv/bin/ruff check .` clean (covers `scripts/` and `tests/`).
   517	4. `wc -l skills/*.md` — each of the four ≤ 150.
   518	5. Zero `src/` diffs (`mypy src/` untouched by construction).
   519	6. Full `./verify` at integration: item 35 flips to PASS; no other item regresses.

    59	## 4. Ticket lifecycle and the write model
    60	
    61	### 4.1 States
    62	
    63	`needs_success → needs_approach → needs_plan → in_progress → needs_review → done`, plus `dropped` (terminal, reachable from any non-`done` state, human-only action).
    64	
    65	There is no stored "ready" or "satisfied" state. Derived views (Section 4.5) express them.
    66	
    67	### 4.2 Fields as structured objects
    68	
    69	`tickets.fields` is a JSON object with exactly four keys: `success`, `approach`, `plan`, `result`. Each is `{value: string|null, proposal: {body, proposed_by, created_at}|null, notes: string|null}`. `value` is canonical and only ever written by the resolution engine (Section 4.4). `notes` is free-guidance text ("when doing the approach, consider X"), writable directly by human or agent at any time (event-logged, no state effect).
    70	
    71	Each pre-terminal state has one **gating field**:
    72	
    73	| state | gating field | accepted proposal advances to |
    74	|---|---|---|
    75	| needs_success | success | needs_approach |
    76	| needs_approach | approach | needs_plan |
    77	| needs_plan | plan | in_progress |
    78	| in_progress | result | needs_review (or done, Section 4.4) |
    79	| needs_review | — (human approval of `result.value`) | done |
    80	
    81	The `result.notes` slot is the ticket's **review notes**: the specific things that need careful checking, written during planning and updated as review happens. Review itself is not a new mechanism: in `needs_review`, an agent orchestrates the Codex CLI against the result and these notes, fixes what review surfaces, and updates the notes — then the human approves. No extra states, entities, or flow machinery for this.
    82	
    83	### 4.3 Ceiling and at-cap
    84	
    85	- The agent grant is two things, always together: **how far** (`ceiling`) and **what happens there** (`at_cap`). These are the whole vocabulary of agent permission.
    86	- `ceiling` is the highest state an agent-caused advancement may move the ticket **into**. Below the ceiling, agents work freely: proposals on the current gating field auto-accept and advance (Section 4.4). Human actions ignore the ceiling entirely (the human may jump a ticket to any state, including straight to `done`; skipped gating fields simply keep `value = null`).
    87	- `at_cap` governs the ticket once it sits at its ceiling: `stop` — agents may not file any proposal; the ticket rests untouched until the human changes the grant. `propose` — agents may file a proposal on the current gating field only; it parks for the human; nothing advances.
    88	- Defaults at creation: `ceiling = needs_success`, `at_cap = propose` (an agent may draft a success condition for approval; nothing advances) [RULING R2 default].
    89	
    90	### 4.4 The resolution engine
    91	
    92	The only write path for `fields.*.value` and for agent-caused state changes. Semantics, exactly:
    93	
    94	1. An agent files a proposal on a field (CLI/API). At most one pending proposal per field: a new proposal replaces the pending one (replacement logged as `proposal_superseded`).
    95	2. If the field is the current state's gating field AND the advance-target state ≤ `ceiling`: the proposal auto-accepts — `value` ← proposal body, proposal cleared, state advances one step, events `proposal_accepted` + `state_changed` logged with `resolved_by: "auto"`.
    96	3. Otherwise the proposal stays pending. Pending proposals on the current gating field put the ticket in the human approval queue.
    97	4. Human resolution of a pending proposal: **accept** (value ← body), **edit** (value ← human-edited text; logged as accepted with `edited: true`), or leave it and discuss in chat. There is no reject action; a proposal can be superseded or the ticket dropped.
    98	5. Special case `in_progress`: an accepted `result` advances to `needs_review` unless `ceiling = done`, in which case it advances directly to `done`. Human approval in `needs_review` (a dedicated approve action, not a field proposal) sets `done`.
    99	6. Every transition writes exactly one `state_changed` event `{from, to, cause}`. One canonical function per transition edge in the code; no second writer.
   100	7. **The onward grant.** Human acceptance of a gating-field proposal (accept or edit-accept) requires the explicit grant pair: `next_ceiling` — any state at or beyond the newly entered state, or `none` meaning the ceiling becomes exactly the newly entered state — AND `at_cap` (`stop`: agents don't even propose from here; `propose`: agents may draft the next gating field for approval). Both are stated on every accept, even when the answer is "nothing further"; an accept without the pair is rejected with a structured error. Agent auto-accepts change neither ceiling nor at_cap. The `needs_review` approve action and sprint-item status accepts carry no onward grant (they are terminal for agent involvement).
   101	
   102	### 4.5 Derived views (must exist as queryable API endpoints, not stored columns)
   103	
   104	- **Approval queue**: tickets with a pending proposal on their current gating field (plus sprint items with pending status proposals, plus `needs_review` tickets). Ordered oldest-pending first.
   105	- **Pickup queue** (dispatcher eligibility): Section 7.2.
   106	- **Overdue**: tickets/items with `deadline` < current planning date and not `done`/`dropped`.
   107	
   108	## 5. Sprints — behavior rules
   109	
   110	- Creating a sprint with kickoff fields sets nothing frozen; an explicit `freeze-kickoff` action sets `kickoff_frozen_at`. Same pattern for review. Frozen-field writes are rejected with a structured error.
   111	- Sprint view data: items grouped by status, each with its ticket rollup (counts by ticket state), plus a **loose tickets** section: tickets whose `sprint_id` = this sprint and `sprint_item_id` IS NULL.
   112	
   113	## 6. Days, the boundary, and the day plan
   114	
   115	### 6.1 Planning date
   116	
   117	Boundary hour: **05:00 local** [RULING R1 default]. Planning date = calendar date of (now − 5 hours). At 04:59 local on July 5 the planning date is July 4; at 05:00 it is July 5. Every "today" in the system uses this rule. In test mode (Section 13) the clock is injectable.
   118	
   119	### 6.2 The boundary job
   120	
   121	Runs once per planning date, at the first scheduler tick at/after the boundary hour (and never twice for the same date; a `boundary_runs` table records completions). It has a deterministic pass (pure code) and a judgment pass (agent adapter):
   122	
   123	Deterministic pass:
   124	1. Materialize the new day row.
   125	2. Compute carryover candidates: yesterday's day-tickets whose ticket state is not `done`/`dropped`.
   126	3. Compute overdue list and the approval-queue digest.
   127	4. Close out yesterday: log a `day_closed` event with a summary payload {counts of done/not-done day tickets}.
   128	
   129	Judgment pass (via the **boundary agent adapter**, one call): given the deterministic outputs, it returns `{brief_markdown, plan_tree}`. The result is stored as the day's `brief` and a **proposed** plan (Section 6.3). If the adapter fails or times out (60s), the day still exists with an empty brief and no plan; the failure is event-logged. v1 evidence is DB-internal only — no repo or session scanning [RULING R4 default].
   130	
   131	If the human has already planned the day explicitly (any day-ticket or accepted plan exists) before the boundary job's judgment pass, the judgment pass is skipped entirely.
   132	
   133	### 6.3 The day-plan tree
   134	
   135	`day.plan` is JSON: `{root: {focus: string, status}, children: [{ticket_id, note, status, position}]}` — one level of children in v1. Node `status` ∈ {`proposed`, `accepted`, `invalidated`}.
   136	
   137	- Accepting a node sets it `accepted`. **Accept-all** accepts root and all children; accepting the root alone does not cascade. When a child with a `ticket_id` is accepted, the ticket is added to the day's ticket list (at the end) if not already present.
   138	- Invalidating the root sets root and every child `invalidated` and triggers one replan-adapter call that returns a complete new tree (replacing the old; old tree preserved in the event payload). Invalidating a child replans only that node: the adapter returns a replacement child. Replans serialize; if an invalidation arrives while a replan is in flight, the in-flight result is discarded and one new replan runs (latest wins) [RULING R5 default].
   139	- **Reject-all**: clears `plan` to null (event-logged); the human plans manually.
   140	
   141	## 7. The dispatcher and runs
   142	
   143	### 7.1 Tick
   144	
   145	Every 60s (configurable), inside the server process. Order within a tick: (1) reclaim expired claims and detect dead worker processes; (2) recompute eligibility; (3) spawn up to the concurrency cap. A machine-wide advisory file lock (`data/dispatcher.lock`) guarantees a single dispatcher; a `dispatch_enabled` config flag is re-read every tick and fails safe to `false` on config read error.
   146	
   147	### 7.2 Eligibility and ordering
   148	
   149	A ticket is eligible iff ALL of: not `done`/`dropped`/`needs_review`; not blocked (3.6); not `auto_blocked`; no active claim; and an agent can do work on it — meaning the current gating field has no pending proposal AND either (a) the advance-target ≤ `ceiling` (the agent can move it), or (b) the ticket is at its ceiling with `at_cap = propose` (the agent can draft the gating field for approval). A ticket at its ceiling with `at_cap = stop` is never eligible. Ordering: priority (P0 first), then deadline ascending with NULLs last, then `created_at` ascending.
   150	
   151	### 7.3 Claims and runs
   152	
   153	- Claim = CAS: `UPDATE tickets SET claim_lock=?, claim_expires=? WHERE id=? AND claim_lock IS NULL`; rowcount 0 = lost. TTL 15 minutes. Every claim inserts a `runs` row (`ticket_id`, `status` ∈ {running, done, blocked, crashed, timed_out, reclaimed}, `started_at`, `ended_at`, `summary`, `error`, `pid`).
   154	- Heartbeat (CLI verb) extends `claim_expires` by one TTL. Expired claim or dead PID → run closed `reclaimed`, lock cleared.
   155	- Max concurrent runs: 2 [RULING R7 default]. Per-run max runtime: 30 minutes → SIGTERM, run `timed_out`.
   156	
   157	### 7.4 Spawning
   158	
   159	Through a **spawn adapter** (subprocess boundary, mockable): `hermes -p <profile> --skills <skill> chat -q "work planning ticket <id>"`, detached session, per-run log file under `data/logs/`, env: `PLAN_SERVER_URL`, `PLAN_TICKET_ID`, `PLAN_RUN_ID`, `PLAN_CLAIM`. Profile and skill names come from config [RULING R3 default: profile `default`, skill `planning-worker`]. Tests never spawn real processes.
   160	
   161	### 7.5 Circuit breaker
   162	
   163	A run ending `crashed`, `timed_out`, or `spawn_failed` increments `tickets.consecutive_failures`; a run ending `done` resets it to 0. Reaching 2 sets `auto_blocked = true` (sticky; event `auto_blocked`). The dispatcher never picks up an auto-blocked ticket; a human clear-action resets both flag and counter.
   164	
   165	### 7.6 Server-side write validation
   166	
   167	Every agent write (proposal, recap, heartbeat, run close) must carry `PLAN_RUN_ID` + `PLAN_CLAIM` matching the ticket's active claim, except proposal/recap writes from non-dispatched agent contexts (no claim env — e.g. the planner-main chat agent), which are permitted with the proposer taken from `PLAN_ACTOR` (default `agent`). Human writes happen through the UI/API only — the CLI is an agent tool. A write with a stale/foreign claim is rejected with a structured error naming the mismatch.
   168	
   169	## 8. The CLI (`plan`)
   170	
   171	A single entry point (`plan`, installed via `pip install -e .` console script) speaking HTTP to the server. **The CLI is for agents (and developer debugging); the human never uses it — every human action lives in the UI.** No resolution verbs exist in it: accepting, approving, granting, unblocking, and day-plan decisions are UI/API-only. Global behavior: `--json` on every verb (machine output; stable exit codes: 0 success, 1 validation/domain error, 2 connection error); long text via `--body-file <path>` or `-` for stdin — body text is never passed as an inline argument; env defaults `PLAN_SERVER_URL`, `PLAN_TICKET_ID` (verbs taking a ticket id use it when omitted), `PLAN_RUN_ID`, `PLAN_CLAIM`, `PLAN_ACTOR`.
   172	
   173	Verbs (exact):
   174	- `plan serve` — run the server (foreground).
   175	- `plan seed --source <dir>` / `plan seed --demo` — Section 12.
   176	- `plan ticket create|show|list|set` (`set` for priority/deadline/day-assignment/sprint-assignment only; ceiling and at_cap are not CLI-settable — grants are human, in the UI).
   177	- `plan propose <field>` (body via stdin/file) · `plan recap` (same input rules) · `plan note <field>`.
   178	- `plan item create|show|list|set|propose-status` (`set` limited to the agent-permitted transitions of Section 3.2) · `plan sprint show` · `plan idea create|list`.
   179	- `plan day show [date]|add-ticket|remove-ticket`.
   180	- `plan link add|rm <from> <to> --kind <kind>` · `plan run heartbeat|close --outcome <o> --summary -`.

 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	"""Ticket routes (§9), plus the ticket-anchored links and the ticket-centric
     2	derived views (board, queues). Thin HTTP shells over the stage-3 writers and the
     3	pure read views: every handler is parse -> auth -> writer -> serialize. No route
     4	re-implements a domain rule and no route appends events.
     5	
     6	This module also homes the shared request plumbing (config/clock accessors, the
     7	per-request connection dependency, the transaction context manager, and the enum
     8	marshaller). The other api modules import these from here; the direction is strictly
     9	one-way (sprints/days/dispatch api -> tickets api), and views modules import no api
    10	module at all."""

    65	async def db_conn(request: Request) -> AsyncIterator[sqlite3.Connection]:
    66	    conn = cast(Callable[[], sqlite3.Connection], request.app.state.conn_factory)()
    67	    try:
    68	        yield conn
    69	    finally:
    70	        conn.close()
    71	
    72	
    73	DbConn = Annotated[sqlite3.Connection, Depends(db_conn)]
    74	Ctx = Annotated[RequestContext, Depends(request_context)]
    75	Cfg = Annotated[Config, Depends(get_config)]
    76	Clk = Annotated[Clock, Depends(get_clock)]

    78	
    79	@contextmanager
    80	def txn(conn: sqlite3.Connection) -> Iterator[None]:
    81	    """Wrap a NON-self-transacting writer (days/dispatch/links) so a mid-sequence
    82	    PlannerError rolls back cleanly. Never wrap a ticket/sprint writer — those open
    83	    their own BEGIN IMMEDIATE and would raise 'transaction within a transaction'."""
    84	    conn.execute("BEGIN IMMEDIATE")
    85	    try:
    86	        yield
    87	    except BaseException:
    88	        conn.execute("ROLLBACK")
    89	        raise
    90	    else:
    91	        conn.execute("COMMIT")
    92	
    93	
    94	def parse_enum[E: StrEnum](enum_cls: type[E], raw: str, what: str) -> E:
    95	    try:
    96	        return enum_cls(raw)
    97	    except ValueError:
    98	        raise PlannerError(ErrorCode.validation, f"invalid {what}", {what: raw}) from None

   146	# --- grant marshallers ---------------------------------------------------------
   147	
   148	
   149	def _parse_next_ceiling(raw: str | None) -> NextCeiling | None:
   150	    if raw is None:
   151	        return None
   152	    if raw == NO_FURTHER:
   153	        return NO_FURTHER
   154	    try:
   155	        return TicketState(raw)
   156	    except ValueError:
   157	        raise PlannerError(
   158	            ErrorCode.grant_invalid, "unknown next_ceiling", {"next_ceiling": raw}
   159	        ) from None
   160	
   161	
   162	def _parse_grant_at_cap(raw: str | None) -> AtCap | None:
   163	    if raw is None:
   164	        return None
   165	    try:
   166	        return AtCap(raw)
   167	    except ValueError:
   168	        raise PlannerError(ErrorCode.grant_invalid, "unknown at_cap", {"at_cap": raw}) from None
   169	
   170	
   171	# --- private gap-fill writers (D2; A1: writer-shaped for relocation) ------------
   172	
   173	
   174	def _set_title(conn: sqlite3.Connection, ticket_id: str, title: str, *,
   175	               title_max_chars: int, now: int) -> Ticket:
   176	    admission.validate_title(title, title_max_chars)
   177	    with txn(conn):
   178	        ticket = tickets_data.read_ticket(conn, ticket_id)
   179	        prev = ticket.title
   180	        conn.execute(
   181	            "UPDATE tickets SET title = ?, updated_at = ? WHERE id = ?", (title, now, ticket_id)
   182	        )
   183	        append_event(
   184	            conn,
   185	            ticket_id,
   186	            EventKind.ticket_updated,
   187	            {"field": "title", "from": prev, "to": title},
   188	            now,
   189	        )
   190	        return tickets_data.read_ticket(conn, ticket_id)
   191	
   192	
   193	def _set_project(conn: sqlite3.Connection, ticket_id: str, project: Project | None, *,
   194	                 now: int) -> Ticket:
   195	    with txn(conn):
   196	        ticket = tickets_data.read_ticket(conn, ticket_id)
   197	        if ticket.sprint_item_id is not None:
   198	            raise PlannerError(ErrorCode.validation, "project is derived when parented")
   199	        prev = ticket.project.value if ticket.project is not None else None
   200	        new_value = project.value if project is not None else None
   201	        conn.execute(
   202	            "UPDATE tickets SET project = ?, updated_at = ? WHERE id = ?",
   203	            (new_value, now, ticket_id),
   204	        )
   205	        append_event(
   206	            conn,
   207	            ticket_id,
   208	            EventKind.ticket_updated,
   209	            {"field": "project", "from": prev, "to": new_value},
   210	            now,
   211	        )
   212	        return tickets_data.read_ticket(conn, ticket_id)
   213

   218	@router.post("/tickets")
   219	async def create_ticket(body: CreateTicketBody, conn: DbConn, ctx: Ctx, cfg: Cfg,
   220	                        clk: Clk) -> JsonDict:
   221	    now = clk.now_unix()
   222	    priority = parse_enum(Priority, body.priority, "priority") if body.priority is not None \
   223	        else Priority.P3
   224	    project = parse_enum(Project, body.project, "project") if body.project is not None else None
   225	    ticket = tickets_data.create_ticket(
   226	        conn,
   227	        title=body.title,
   228	        actor=ctx.actor,
   229	        now=now,
   230	        title_max_chars=cfg.title_max_chars,
   231	        project=project,
   232	        priority=priority,
   233	        deadline=body.deadline,
   234	        sprint_id=body.sprint_id,
   235	        sprint_item_id=body.sprint_item_id,
   236	    )
   237	    return tickets_views.ticket_json(ticket, now)

   242	                       project: str | None = None, sprint_id: str | None = None,
   243	                       sprint_item_id: str | None = None) -> JsonDict:
   244	    state_enum = parse_enum(TicketState, state, "state") if state is not None else None
   245	    project_enum = parse_enum(Project, project, "project") if project is not None else None
   246	    return {

   252	            sprint_id=sprint_id,
   253	            sprint_item_id=sprint_item_id,
   254	        )
   255	    }

   262	
   263	@router.patch("/tickets/{ticket_id}")
   264	async def patch_ticket(ticket_id: str, body: dict[str, Any], conn: DbConn, ctx: Ctx,
   265	                       cfg: Cfg, clk: Clk) -> JsonDict:
   266	    recognized = ("title", "priority", "deadline", "project", "sprint_id")
   267	    for key in body:
   268	        if key not in recognized:
   269	            raise PlannerError(ErrorCode.validation, "unknown ticket field", {"field": key})
   270	    if not body:
   271	        raise PlannerError(ErrorCode.validation, "no ticket fields to update", {})
   272	    now = clk.now_unix()
   273	    if "title" in body:
   274	        _set_title(conn, ticket_id, body["title"], title_max_chars=cfg.title_max_chars, now=now)
   275	    if "priority" in body:
   276	        priority = parse_enum(Priority, body["priority"], "priority")
   277	        tickets_data.set_priority(conn, ticket_id, priority=priority, actor=ctx.actor, now=now)
   278	    if "deadline" in body:
   279	        tickets_data.set_deadline(conn, ticket_id, deadline=body["deadline"], actor=ctx.actor,
   280	                                  now=now)
   281	    if "project" in body:
   282	        project = parse_enum(Project, body["project"], "project") \
   283	            if body["project"] is not None else None
   284	        _set_project(conn, ticket_id, project, now=now)
   285	    if "sprint_id" in body:
   286	        tickets_data.set_sprint(conn, ticket_id, sprint_id=body["sprint_id"], actor=ctx.actor,
   287	                                now=now)
   288	    return tickets_views.ticket_json(tickets_data.read_ticket(conn, ticket_id), now)
   289

   291	@router.post("/tickets/{ticket_id}/propose/{field}")
   292	async def propose_field(ticket_id: str, field: str, body: ProposeBody, conn: DbConn, ctx: Ctx,
   293	                        clk: Clk) -> JsonDict:
   294	    field_enum = parse_enum(FieldName, field, "field")
   295	    now = clk.now_unix()
   296	    if ctx.is_claimed_agent:
   297	        require_claim(conn, ctx, ticket_id, now)
   298	    ticket = tickets_data.file_proposal(
   299	        conn, ticket_id, field=field_enum, body=body.body, actor=ctx.actor, now=now
   300	    )
   301	    return tickets_views.ticket_json(ticket, now)

   304	@router.post("/tickets/{ticket_id}/accept/{field}")
   305	async def accept_field(ticket_id: str, field: str, body: AcceptBody, conn: DbConn, ctx: Ctx,
   306	                       clk: Clk) -> JsonDict:
   307	    reject_agents(ctx)
   308	    field_enum = parse_enum(FieldName, field, "field")
   309	    now = clk.now_unix()
   310	    next_ceiling = _parse_next_ceiling(body.next_ceiling)
   311	    at_cap = _parse_grant_at_cap(body.at_cap)
   312	    ticket = tickets_data.accept_proposal(
   313	        conn,
   314	        ticket_id,
   315	        field=field_enum,
   316	        actor=ctx.actor,
   317	        now=now,
   318	        edited_body=body.edited_body,
   319	        next_ceiling=next_ceiling,
   320	        at_cap=at_cap,
   321	    )
   322	    return tickets_views.ticket_json(ticket, now)

   358	                       clk: Clk) -> JsonDict:
   359	    reject_agents(ctx)
   360	    now = clk.now_unix()
   361	    if body.ceiling is None or body.at_cap is None:
   362	        missing = [name for name, raw in (("ceiling", body.ceiling), ("at_cap", body.at_cap))
   363	                   if raw is None]
   364	        raise PlannerError(
   365	            ErrorCode.grant_missing, "grant requires ceiling and at_cap", {"missing": missing}
   366	        )
   367	    try:
   368	        ceiling = TicketState(body.ceiling)
   369	    except ValueError:
   370	        raise PlannerError(
   371	            ErrorCode.grant_invalid, "unknown ceiling", {"ceiling": body.ceiling}
   372	        ) from None
   373	    try:
   374	        at_cap = AtCap(body.at_cap)
   375	    except ValueError:
   376	        raise PlannerError(
   377	            ErrorCode.grant_invalid, "unknown at_cap", {"at_cap": body.at_cap}
   378	        ) from None
   379	    ticket = tickets_data.change_grant(
   380	        conn, ticket_id, ceiling=ceiling, at_cap=at_cap, actor=ctx.actor, now=now
   381	    )
   382	    return tickets_views.ticket_json(ticket, now)
   383	
   384	
   385	@router.post("/tickets/{ticket_id}/state")
   386	async def set_state(ticket_id: str, body: StateBody, conn: DbConn, ctx: Ctx,
   387	                    clk: Clk) -> JsonDict:
   388	    reject_agents(ctx)
   389	    now = clk.now_unix()
   390	    to_state = parse_enum(TicketState, body.to, "state")
   391	    ticket = tickets_data.set_state(conn, ticket_id, new_state=to_state, actor=ctx.actor, now=now)
   392	    return tickets_views.ticket_json(ticket, now)
   393	
   394	
   395	@router.post("/tickets/{ticket_id}/drop")
   396	async def drop_ticket(ticket_id: str, conn: DbConn, ctx: Ctx, clk: Clk) -> JsonDict:
   397	    reject_agents(ctx)
   398	    now = clk.now_unix()
   399	    ticket = tickets_data.drop_ticket(conn, ticket_id, actor=ctx.actor, now=now)
   400	    return tickets_views.ticket_json(ticket, now)

   404	async def unblock_ticket(ticket_id: str, conn: DbConn, ctx: Ctx, clk: Clk) -> JsonDict:
   405	    reject_agents(ctx)
   406	    now = clk.now_unix()

   408	        dispatch_data.clear_auto_block(conn, ticket_id, now)
   409	    return tickets_views.ticket_json(tickets_data.read_ticket(conn, ticket_id), now)

 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	"""The `plan` CLI (§8). A single entry point speaking HTTP to the server. The CLI
     2	is for agents and developer debugging; the human operates through the UI. No
     3	resolution verbs exist here (accept/approve/grant/unblock/day-plan are UI/API
     4	only).
     5	
     6	Every verb supports --json (machine output; exit codes 0 success, 1
     7	validation/domain error, 2 connection error). Long text arrives via
     8	`--body-file <path>` where `-` reads stdin — body text is never an inline
     9	argument. Every handler speaks HTTP through planner.cli.http, which owns all
    10	output and exit-code decisions; a handler only builds a route + body."""
    11	
    12	from __future__ import annotations
    13	
    14	import os
    15	import sqlite3
    16	import sys
    17	from collections.abc import Callable
    18	from pathlib import Path
    19	from typing import Any
    20	
    21	import click
    22	from click.core import ParameterSource
    23	
    24	from planner.cli import http
    25	
    26	_PRIORITIES = ["P0", "P1", "P2", "P3"]
    27	_FIELDS = ["success", "approach", "plan", "result"]
    28	_LINK_KINDS = ["belongs_to", "parent_child", "blocks", "relates"]
    29	_TICKET_ID_ENV = "PLAN_TICKET_ID"
    30	
    31	
    32	# --- body input, id resolution, and render helpers (amendment 6) ---------------
    33	
    34	
    35	def _read_source(spec: str, as_json: bool) -> str:
    36	    """`-` -> stdin; else read the file. Unreadable file -> validation exit 1."""
    37	    if spec == "-":
    38	        return sys.stdin.read()
    39	    try:
    40	        return Path(spec).read_text(encoding="utf-8")
    41	    except OSError:
    42	        http.fail_validation(f"cannot read body file: {spec}", as_json)
    43	
    44	
    45	def _positional_is_stdin_marker(positional: str | None) -> bool:
    46	    """True only for a '-' the caller actually typed. click fills the positional from
    47	    PLAN_TICKET_ID too, and an env value of '-' is a bogus ticket id, not a stdin
    48	    request (amendment 6: body sources are explicit only)."""
    49	    if positional != "-":
    50	        return False
    51	    source = click.get_current_context().get_parameter_source("ticket_id")
    52	    return source == ParameterSource.COMMANDLINE
    53	
    54	
    55	def read_body(positional_ticket_id: str | None, body_file: str | None, as_json: bool) -> str:
    56	    """Required-body verbs. Body source is explicit only: --body-file PATH (or -), or a
    57	    trailing positional that is literally '-' (the stdin marker, not a ticket id).
    58	    Whitespace-only body -> validation exit 1. Returns the original unstripped text."""
    59	    if body_file is not None:
    60	        text = _read_source(body_file, as_json)
    61	    elif _positional_is_stdin_marker(positional_ticket_id):
    62	        text = sys.stdin.read()
    63	    else:
    64	        http.fail_validation("body required: pass '-' for stdin or --body-file PATH", as_json)
    65	    if not text.strip():
    66	        http.fail_validation("empty body", as_json)
    67	    return text
    68	
    69	
    70	def read_optional_body(body_file: str | None, as_json: bool) -> str | None:
    71	    """Optional-body verbs. Body comes only from --body-file (which may be '-');
    72	    absent flag -> None."""
    73	    if body_file is None:
    74	        return None
    75	    return _read_source(body_file, as_json)
    76	
    77	
    78	def resolve_ticket_id(positional: str | None, as_json: bool) -> str:
    79	    """Effective ticket id. A real positional wins; a None or '-' positional falls back
    80	    to PLAN_TICKET_ID (re-read here since click's envvar fallback does not fire when the
    81	    positional is the '-' stdin marker). Missing -> validation exit 1."""
    82	    if positional is not None and positional != "-":
    83	        return positional
    84	    env = os.environ.get(_TICKET_ID_ENV, "").strip()
    85	    if env and env != "-":  # '-' is the stdin marker, never a ticket id
    86	        return env
    87	    http.fail_validation("ticket id required: pass it or set PLAN_TICKET_ID", as_json)
    88	
    89	
    90	def _drop_none(d: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:
    91	    return {k: v for k, v in d.items() if v is not None}
    92	
    93	
    94	def _lines(rows: list[Any], fmt: Callable[[Any], str]) -> str:
    95	    return "\n".join(fmt(r) for r in rows) if rows else "(none)"
    96	
    97	
    98	def _seed_human(data: Any) -> str:
    99	    lines = [
   100	        f"seed: {data['sprints']} sprint(s), {data['sprint_items']} item(s), "
   101	        f"{data['deferred_items']} deferred, {data['tickets']} ticket(s), "
   102	        f"{data['ideas']} idea(s), {data['links']} link(s), "
   103	        f"{data['duplicates_skipped']} duplicate(s)"
   104	    ]
   105	    skipped = data["skipped"]
   106	    if skipped:
   107	        lines.append(f"skipped: {len(skipped)}")
   108	        for s in skipped:
   109	            lines.append(f"  - {s['source_file']} [{s['heading']}]: {s['reason']}")
   110	    return "\n".join(lines)
   111	
   112	
   113	def _require_run_id(as_json: bool) -> str:
   114	    rid = os.environ.get("PLAN_RUN_ID", "").strip()
   115	    if not rid:
   116	        http.fail_validation("run id required: set PLAN_RUN_ID", as_json)
   117	    return rid
   118	
   119	
   120	def json_option(func: Callable[..., Any]) -> Callable[..., Any]:
   121	    return click.option(
   122	        "--json",
   123	        "as_json",
   124	        is_flag=True,
   125	        default=False,
   126	        help="Emit machine-readable JSON (errors as JSON on stderr).",
   127	    )(func)
   128	
   129	
   130	@click.group()
   131	def main() -> None:
   132	    """plan — the planner CLI for agents and developer debugging."""
   133	
   134	
   135	# --- serve (the one handler that actually works in T01) ---
   136	
   137	
   138	@main.command("serve")
   139	@json_option
   140	def serve(as_json: bool) -> None:
   141	    """Run the server (foreground)."""
   142	    import os
   143	
   144	    import uvicorn
   145	
   146	    from planner.core.adapters.registry import build_adapters
   147	    from planner.core.clock import build_clock
   148	    from planner.core.config import HOST, load_config
   149	    from planner.core.db import connect, create_schema
   150	    from planner.core.server import create_app
   151	
   152	    config = load_config()
   153	    os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(config.db_path) or ".", exist_ok=True)
   154	    os.makedirs(config.logs_dir, exist_ok=True)
   155	    with connect(config.db_path, config.db_busy_timeout_ms) as bootstrap:
   156	        create_schema(bootstrap)
   157	
   158	    clock = build_clock(config)
   159	    adapters = build_adapters(config)
   160	
   161	    def conn_factory() -> sqlite3.Connection:
   162	        return connect(config.db_path, config.db_busy_timeout_ms)
   163	
   164	    app = create_app(config, clock, adapters, conn_factory)
   165	    uvicorn.run(app, host=HOST, port=config.port)
   166

   170	
   171	@main.command("seed")
   172	@click.option("--source", default=None, help="Path to a markdown planning directory.")
   173	@click.option("--demo", is_flag=True, default=False, help="Create the deterministic demo dataset.")
   174	@json_option
   175	def seed(source: str | None, demo: bool, as_json: bool) -> None:
   176	    """Import a markdown planning directory, or create a demo dataset (§12)."""
   177	    if (source is None) == (not demo):
   178	        http.fail_validation("provide exactly one of --source or --demo", as_json)
   179	    body: dict[str, Any]
   180	    if demo:
   181	        body = {"demo": True}
   182	    else:
   183	        assert source is not None
   184	        body = {"source_dir": os.path.abspath(source)}  # server resolves in ITS cwd
   185	    data = http.send("POST", "/api/seed", as_json=as_json, json_body=body)
   186	    http.emit(data, as_json, _seed_human(data))

   189	# --- top-level proposal/recap/note verbs ---

   192	@main.command("propose")
   193	@click.argument("field", type=click.Choice(_FIELDS))
   194	@click.argument("ticket_id", required=False, envvar=_TICKET_ID_ENV)
   195	@click.option("--body-file", default=None, help="Path to the body, or - for stdin.")
   196	@json_option
   197	def propose(field: str, ticket_id: str | None, body_file: str | None, as_json: bool) -> None:
   198	    """File or replace a proposal on a field (agent path)."""
   199	    body = read_body(ticket_id, body_file, as_json)
   200	    tid = resolve_ticket_id(ticket_id, as_json)
   201	    data = http.send(
   202	        "POST", f"/api/tickets/{tid}/propose/{field}", as_json=as_json, json_body={"body": body}
   203	    )
   204	    http.emit(data, as_json, f"proposed {field} on {data['id']}")

   212	    """Write a ticket recap (rejected before needs_approach)."""
   213	    body = read_body(ticket_id, body_file, as_json)
   214	    tid = resolve_ticket_id(ticket_id, as_json)
   215	    data = http.send("PUT", f"/api/tickets/{tid}/recap", as_json=as_json, json_body={"body": body})
   216	    http.emit(data, as_json, f"recap written on {data['id']}")

   239	    """Ticket verbs."""

   241	
   242	@ticket.command("create")
   243	@click.option("--title", required=True, help="Ticket title (<= 200 chars).")
   244	@click.option("--priority", type=click.Choice(_PRIORITIES), default=None)
   245	@click.option("--deadline", default=None, help="ISO date.")
   246	@click.option("--project", default=None, help="Vylo | Tribe | Learning | Other.")
   247	@click.option("--sprint", default=None, help="Sprint id (standalone tickets only).")
   248	@click.option("--item", default=None, help="Sprint item id to parent under.")
   249	@json_option
   250	def ticket_create(
   251	    title: str,
   252	    priority: str | None,
   253	    deadline: str | None,
   254	    project: str | None,
   255	    sprint: str | None,
   256	    item: str | None,
   257	    as_json: bool,
   258	) -> None:
   259	    """Create a ticket."""
   260	    body: dict[str, Any] = {"title": title}
   261	    if priority is not None:
   262	        body["priority"] = priority
   263	    if deadline is not None:
   264	        body["deadline"] = deadline
   265	    if project is not None:
   266	        body["project"] = project
   267	    if sprint is not None:
   268	        body["sprint_id"] = sprint       # --sprint -> sprint_id
   269	    if item is not None:
   270	        body["sprint_item_id"] = item    # --item   -> sprint_item_id
   271	    data = http.send("POST", "/api/tickets", as_json=as_json, json_body=body)
   272	    http.emit(data, as_json, f"{data['id']} {data['state']}")

   278	def ticket_show(ticket_id: str | None, as_json: bool) -> None:
   279	    """Show a ticket (defaults to $PLAN_TICKET_ID)."""

   281	    data = http.send("GET", f"/api/tickets/{tid}", as_json=as_json)
   282	    http.emit(data, as_json, f"{data['id']} {data['state']} {data['priority']} {data['title']}")

   285	@ticket.command("list")
   286	@click.option("--state", default=None)
   287	@click.option("--project", default=None)
   288	@click.option("--sprint", default=None)
   289	@json_option
   290	def ticket_list(state: str | None, project: str | None, sprint: str | None, as_json: bool) -> None:
   291	    """List tickets."""
   292	    params = _drop_none({"state": state, "project": project, "sprint_id": sprint})
   293	    data = http.send("GET", "/api/tickets", as_json=as_json, params=params)
   294	    http.emit(
   295	        data,
   296	        as_json,
   297	        _lines(data["tickets"], lambda t: f"{t['id']} {t['state']} {t['priority']} {t['title']}"),
   298	    )

   300	
   301	@ticket.command("set")
   302	@click.argument("ticket_id", required=False, envvar=_TICKET_ID_ENV)
   303	@click.option("--priority", type=click.Choice(_PRIORITIES), default=None)
   304	@click.option("--deadline", default=None, help="ISO date, or 'none' to clear.")
   305	@click.option("--day", default=None, help="ISO date or 'today' to assign to a day.")
   306	@click.option("--sprint", default=None, help="Sprint id, or 'none' to clear.")
   307	@json_option
   308	def ticket_set(
   309	    ticket_id: str | None,
   310	    priority: str | None,
   311	    deadline: str | None,
   312	    day: str | None,
   313	    sprint: str | None,
   314	    as_json: bool,
   315	) -> None:
   316	    """Set priority / deadline / day / sprint (no ceiling or at_cap — grants are human)."""
   317	    tid = resolve_ticket_id(ticket_id, as_json)
   318	    patch: dict[str, Any] = {}
   319	    if priority is not None:
   320	        patch["priority"] = priority
   321	    if deadline is not None:
   322	        patch["deadline"] = None if deadline == "none" else deadline
   323	    if sprint is not None:
   324	        patch["sprint_id"] = None if sprint == "none" else sprint
   325	    if not patch and day is None:
   326	        http.fail_validation("nothing to set: pass --priority/--deadline/--sprint/--day", as_json)
   327	    data: Any = None
   328	    if patch:
   329	        data = http.send("PATCH", f"/api/tickets/{tid}", as_json=as_json, json_body=patch)
   330	    if day is not None:
   331	        data = http.send(
   332	            "POST", f"/api/day/{day}/tickets", as_json=as_json, json_body={"ticket_id": tid}
   333	        )
   334	    # `data` is the LAST successful response: ticket_json if only PATCH, day view if --day.
   335	    human = (
   336	        f"day {data['id']}: {len(data['tickets'])} ticket(s)"
   337	        if "tickets" in data
   338	        else f"{data['id']} {data['state']}"
   339	    )
   340	    http.emit(data, as_json, human)
   341

   387	@item.command("list")

   389	@click.option("--project", default=None)
   390	@click.option("--backlog", is_flag=True, default=False, help="Items with no sprint (backlog).")
   391	@json_option
   392	def item_list(status: str | None, project: str | None, backlog: bool, as_json: bool) -> None:
   393	    """List sprint items."""
   394	    params = _drop_none({"status": status, "project": project})
   395	    if backlog:
   396	        params["sprint_id"] = "null"        # views.list_items maps "null" -> IS NULL
   397	    data = http.send("GET", "/api/items", as_json=as_json, params=params)
   398	    http.emit(
   399	        data,
   400	        as_json,
   401	        _lines(data["items"], lambda i: f"{i['id']} {i['status']} {i['priority']} {i['title']}"),
   402	    )

   405	@item.command("set")
   406	@click.argument("item_id")
   407	@click.option("--status", type=click.Choice(["todo", "active", "blocked"]), default=None)
   408	@click.option("--blocked-by", default=None, help="Comma-separated blocker ticket ids.")
   409	@json_option
   410	def item_set(item_id: str, status: str | None, blocked_by: str | None, as_json: bool) -> None:
   411	    """Set an agent-permitted item transition (§3.2)."""
   412	    body: dict[str, Any] = {}
   413	    if status is not None:
   414	        body["status"] = status
   415	    if blocked_by is not None:
   416	        body["blocked_by"] = [s for s in (x.strip() for x in blocked_by.split(",")) if s]
   417	    if not body:
   418	        http.fail_validation("nothing to set: pass --status/--blocked-by", as_json)
   419	    data = http.send("PATCH", f"/api/items/{item_id}", as_json=as_json, json_body=body)
   420	    http.emit(data, as_json, f"{data['id']} {data['status']}")
   421

   427	)
   428	@click.option("--body-file", default=None, help="Optional rationale body, or - for stdin.")
   429	@json_option
   430	def item_propose_status(item_id: str, to_status: str, body_file: str | None, as_json: bool) -> None:
   431	    """Propose a done / deferred_next_sprint status for human acceptance."""
   432	    note = read_optional_body(body_file, as_json)
   433	    data = http.send(
   434	        "POST", f"/api/items/{item_id}/propose-status",
   435	        as_json=as_json, json_body={"to": to_status, "note": note},

   241	## 17. Skills artifacts
   242	
   243	Write four skill documents under `skills/` (not installed anywhere by the build): `planning-worker.md` (how a dispatched agent works a ticket via the CLI: orient with `plan ticket show --json`, propose via stdin, recap discipline, heartbeats, never asking questions), `planning-boundary.md` (the judgment pass contract), `planner-main.md` (the day/system chat agent: operating the queues and day plan via CLI, and quick capture — anything the human tosses into the day chat that is work or an idea gets filed as a standalone ticket or idea via the CLI, immediately, defaulting to P3), `planning-executor.md` (working an in_progress ticket you own, including the review discipline: at needs_review, orchestrate the Codex CLI against the result and the review notes, fix what it surfaces, update the notes). Each ≤ 150 lines, written for the agent that will load it. DOCS.md summarizes their roles.
   244	
   245	---
   246	
   247	## 18. Delivery expectations and acceptance checklist
   248	
   249	**Build in this order**: (1) contracts skeleton — schema DDL, enums, typed models, adapter interfaces, config loading, CLI/API surface stubs; (2) **the verify instrument** (18.2), runnable with every checklist item FAIL; (3) pure-logic modules + unit tests (items 1–21 and 36) green; (4) server wiring — API, WS, resolution engine over DB, dispatcher, boundary scheduler, CLI against the API; (5) UI views; (6) Playwright e2e suite, items 22–34 green; (7) the fake-ticket dogfood pass (Section 18.5): Level A green (item 35), Levels B and C executed with evidence recorded in DOGFOOD.md. Do not start a later stage while an earlier stage's tests fail.
   250	
   251	Where this document delegates a choice, make it, implement it fully, and record it in decisions.md. If a genuine contradiction emerges, state it in PROGRESS.md and propose a resolution consistent with Section 14's rules — do not silently pick.
   252	
   253	### 18.1 Required artifacts
   254	
   255	- **PROGRESS.md** — updated every work cycle: current stage, what just passed, current hypothesis, next step, blockers. First read after any context compaction. Three failed attempts on one problem → log it and change approach materially.
   256	- **decisions.md** — every delegated choice, briefly justified.
   257	- **DOCS.md** — plain-language documentation written progressively as each stage completes, never retrofitted. A smart non-engineer must be able to read it: what the planner is, the entities, how proposals and ceilings work, what the dispatcher does, how a day flows, what each screen shows, what the CLI verbs do, what the skills are for. If a section needs the code to be understood, rewrite the section.
   258	- **DOGFOOD.md** — the record of the Section 18.5 fake-ticket dogfood: per level, what was run, the exact commands, the evidence (event-log excerpts, run rows, session/log file paths under `data/logs/`), and an honest outcome. Narrative without corresponding logs is not evidence.
   259	
   260	### 18.2 The verify instrument
   261	
   262	- One command: `./verify` (executable script at repo root). Runs in order: ruff, mypy, unit suite, build check (`python -m compileall src/` + `node --check` on every file in `assets/`), then the Playwright e2e suite headless. Prints a per-item scoreboard for every acceptance test below (item number, name, PASS/FAIL) and ends with exactly `VERIFY: N/36 PASS`.
   263	- The instrument fails with an explicit message if any test file contains `@pytest.mark.skip`, `pytest.skip(`, `xfail`, `.only`, a commented-out test, or an empty test body — the scan itself is unit-tested (item 21).
   264	- Built at stage 2, before implementation; all items report FAIL until their stage lands. The scoreboard is the single source of truth for completeness.
   265	- Unit items are 1–21 and 36; end-to-end items are 22–35. Item 34 (snapshot migration) runs against `migration/source-snapshot/`, and item 35 (dogfood Level A) is the scripted walkthrough — both part of the suite, not manual steps. Levels B and C of the dogfood (Section 18.5) are deliberately OUTSIDE `./verify`: they use live agents and belong to stage 7's evidence, not the deterministic gate.
   266	
   267	### 18.3 Acceptance tests
   268	
   269	**Fences:**
   270	- Each item maps 1:1 to a named test whose name includes the item number (`test_a01_...`, `test_e22_...`).
   271	- Assertions use the specific values in this document — exact states, exact orderings, exact error shapes — not weakened approximations.
   272	- After first written, any change to a test requires a logged justification in decisions.md.
   273	- A claim of passing is valid only if `./verify` ran fresh in the same turn with full output shown.
   274	
   275	External boundaries (hermes spawn, boundary/replan agent, chat gateway) are faked in all tests. E2E uses a real server + temp SQLite DB + `PLAN_TEST_MODE=1`, driving ticks via the test endpoints and time via `PLAN_FAKE_NOW`. Multi-surface behavior uses two browser contexts.
   276	
   277	**Unit (pytest, pure logic + engine over a temp DB):**
   278	1. Planning-date math: 2026-07-05T04:59 local → planning date 2026-07-04; T05:00 → 2026-07-05; boundary hour honored from config.
   279	2. Gating chain: accepting success/approach/plan/result proposals advances exactly one state each, in order, with one `state_changed` event per step.
   280	3. Ceiling auto-accept: ceiling `needs_plan` with `at_cap = propose`, proposals filed on success then approach auto-accept and advance; the plan proposal stays pending (state `needs_plan`, ticket in approval queue).
   281	4. At-cap stop vs propose: at its ceiling with `at_cap = stop`, any agent proposal — including the current gating field — is rejected with the structured error; with `at_cap = propose`, a proposal on the current gating field files as pending and nothing advances; below the ceiling the behavior is identical for both values.
   282	5. One pending proposal per field: a second proposal supersedes the first; `proposal_superseded` event carries the replaced body.
   283	6. Edit-accept: accepting with edited text stores the edited text exactly as `value`, flags `edited: true` in the event.
   284	7. Result routing: accepted result with ceiling `needs_review` → state `needs_review`; with ceiling `done` → `done`; approve action in `needs_review` → `done`.
   285	8. Recap: write at `needs_success` rejected with structured error; write at `needs_approach` succeeds, overwrites prior recap, logs event, changes no state.
   286	9. Blocking: ticket blocked by an open ticket is dispatch-ineligible; blocker → `done` makes it eligible; `blocks` cycle creation rejected.
   287	10. Sprint-item permissions: agent `todo→active` succeeds; agent direct `active→done` write rejected; `done` via proposal + accept succeeds; `blocked_by` list stored and `blockers_cleared` computed when all blockers done.
   288	11. Dispatch ordering: fixture of five eligible tickets orders P0 before P1; equal priority by earlier deadline; NULL deadline last; then `created_at`.
   289	12. Day-ticket removal: deletes association only, position list re-packs contiguously, ticket state unchanged, `day_ticket_removed` logged.
   290	13. Sprint assignment rules: standalone ticket accepts `sprint_id`; parented ticket's `sprint_id` write rejected; parent's sprint derived.
   291	14. Claim CAS: two concurrent claim attempts on one ticket → exactly one wins, one `runs` row created.
   292	15. TTL/reclaim: expired claim reclaimed (run `reclaimed`, lock cleared, eligible again); heartbeat extends expiry by one TTL.
   293	16. Circuit breaker: two consecutive `crashed` runs → `auto_blocked`, ineligible; unblock action clears flag and counter; a `done` run resets the counter.
   294	17. Day-plan tree: root invalidation marks root + all children invalidated and requests one replan; child invalidation replaces only that child; accept-all accepts every node and adds child tickets to the day list exactly once.
   295	18. Boundary job (fake clock): first tick ≥ 05:00 creates the day, computes carryover (yesterday's non-done day tickets), overdue list, logs `day_closed`; second tick same date does nothing; explicit prior planning skips the judgment pass.
   296	19. Seed fixtures: importing `tests/fixtures/planning-md/` yields the exact expected entity counts and spot-checked values (item statuses, ticket states per the Readiness mapping, preserved Chat ID, deferred items with NULL sprint, idea with project); re-running seeds zero duplicates.
   297	20. Freeze rules: kickoff write after freeze rejected; `weekly_addenda` append still allowed; review same pattern. Sprint overlap rejected.
   298	21. Instrument integrity: the skip-scan detects each forbidden pattern (`@pytest.mark.skip`, `pytest.skip(`, `xfail`, empty body) in a synthetic file set and passes a clean set; `PLAN_FAKE_NOW` is ignored when `PLAN_TEST_MODE` is unset.
   299	
   300	**End-to-end (Playwright, chromium, two browser contexts where stated):**
   301	22. CLI create → live board: `plan ticket create` via subprocess with env pointing at the test server; the ticket appears on the Board in `needs_success` in a second context without reload.
   302	23. Env-pinned propose: `plan propose success` with `PLAN_TICKET_ID` set and a multi-line markdown body on stdin; Ticket screen shows the proposal rendered, intact.
   303	24. Accept in Review: the pending proposal appears on the Review screen; Accept is impossible without choosing the grant pair; choosing ("no further", stop) advances the ticket one state with ceiling equal to the new state and `at_cap = stop` (asserted via API); the item leaves the queue and both contexts update without reload.
   304	25. Edit-accept in Review: the quick-edit textarea comes prefilled with the proposal; submitting altered text with grant (`needs_plan`, propose) stores the altered text exactly as the field value on Ticket, sets the ceiling to `needs_plan` and `at_cap` to propose.
   305	26. Chat panel: opens on Ticket with the fake gateway (echo); a sent message renders a reply; `chat_session_key` persisted (asserted via API); gateway-offline fake renders the offline notice.
   306	27. Auto-accept chain e2e: ticket with ceiling `needs_plan` and `at_cap = propose`: two CLI proposals advance it to `needs_plan` with the plan proposal pending in the approval queue.
   307	28. Day view: with a fake boundary adapter, driving `POST /api/test/tick-boundary` at fake-now 05:01 produces the brief and a proposed tree; Accept-all adds the child tickets to the day list.
   308	29. Invalidation: invalidating the root calls the fake replan adapter and renders the replacement tree; invalidating one child replaces only that child (other nodes keep status).
   309	30. Dispatcher e2e: eligible ticket + fake spawn adapter; `POST /api/test/tick-dispatcher` claims it (run visible on Ticket); the fake worker's CLI calls (propose result with claim env) park it in `needs_review`; it appears in the approval queue; approve → `done`.
   310	31. Refresh restores state: on Day, Board, and Ticket mid-flow (pending proposal, running claim), a reload renders identical content.
   311	32. Sprint view live: `plan item set --status active` via CLI reflects on the Sprint screen in both contexts without reload; loose ticket appears in the loose section.
   312	33. Seed e2e: `plan seed --source tests/fixtures/planning-md/` against the test server, then Board/Sprint/Backlog show the fixture's expected titles and counts.
   313	34. Snapshot migration e2e: `plan seed --source migration/source-snapshot/` imports the frozen real data with exactly the ground truth stated in Section 12 (1 sprint with those dates; 12 items split 6/5/1 with "Ship waitlist mechanics." done; the 4 named tickets in their mapped states with the Chat ID preserved; 9 deferred items; 20 ideas); the migration report lists zero silently-skipped sections (anything unparsed is enumerated in it); a second run imports zero duplicates.
   314	35. Dogfood Level A: `scripts/dogfood_cli.py` drives the full workflow against a test server seeded with `plan seed --demo` — agent actions through the real CLI, human actions (accepts with grant pairs, needs_review approve, day-plan decisions) through the HTTP API, since the CLI deliberately has no human verbs: create a ticket; propose and accept through every gate — every accept carrying its grant pair — including one edit-accept and one superseded proposal; write and overwrite the recap; assign to the day and remove; create a blocking link and confirm dispatch-ineligibility, then complete the blocker and confirm eligibility; claim via the test dispatcher tick; propose result with claim env; approve from needs_review to done. The script asserts each step's resulting state via `--json`/response output and exits non-zero on the first mismatch.
   315	36. Onward grant (unit): a human accept missing either half of the grant pair is rejected with the structured error and changes nothing; accept with (`none`, `stop`) leaves the ticket resting — a subsequent agent proposal is rejected; accept with (`none`, `propose`) permits a pending proposal on the new gating field; accept with a later ceiling permits advancement to it; agent auto-accepts change neither ceiling nor at_cap; the `needs_review` approve action requires no grant.
   316	
   317	### 18.5 Fake-ticket dogfood (stage 7)
   318	
   319	The application must be proven with realistic fake tickets at three escalating levels of automation. This is the one sanctioned exception to the fakes-only rule: Levels B and C use the real local Hermes runtime — never inside `./verify`, always recorded in DOGFOOD.md with log evidence.
   320	
   321	- **Level A — the CLI as a user and agent would drive it.** The scripted walkthrough of item 35. Deterministic, gating, part of `./verify`.
   322	- **Level B — a Hermes agent works a fake ticket.** Spawn one real session: `hermes chat -q "Read <repo>/skills/planning-worker.md, then work planning ticket <id> using the plan CLI"` with the agent env vars pinned (server URL + ticket id) against a running server with a demo-seeded ticket shaped for it (ceiling `needs_plan`, `at_cap = propose`). Pass evidence, mechanical: at least one proposal and one recap write recorded on that ticket by the session (asserted from the event log), session output captured under `data/logs/` and referenced in DOGFOOD.md. The repo's skill artifacts are exercised via the read-this-file prompt because they are not installed into `~/.hermes/skills/` during the run.

     1	"""§4.4 resolution semantics as pure Decisions. _accept_gating_proposal is the
     2	sole constructor of any Decision that traverses the gating-acceptance edges 1-5
     3	(auto and human alike); _state_change is the sole constructor of state_changed
     4	EventSpecs. Together with data._apply_decision (the sole appender) this is the
     5	"one canonical writer per transition" guarantee (§4.4.6)."""
     6	
     7	from __future__ import annotations
     8	
     9	from typing import Final
    10	
    11	from planner.core.contracts import EventKind
    12	from planner.core.errors import ErrorCode, PlannerError
    13	from planner.tickets.contracts import (
    14	    AtCap,
    15	    FieldName,
    16	    FieldSlot,
    17	    GrantPair,

    19	    Proposal,

    23	from planner.tickets.logic import admission, fields_codec, machine
    24	from planner.tickets.logic.decisions import Decision, EventSpec

    26	CAUSE_AUTO_ACCEPT: Final[str] = "auto_accept"
    27	CAUSE_HUMAN_ACCEPT: Final[str] = "human_accept"
    28	CAUSE_REVIEW_APPROVE: Final[str] = "review_approve"
    29	CAUSE_HUMAN_STATE_JUMP: Final[str] = "human_state_jump"
    30	CAUSE_DROP: Final[str] = "drop"
    31	CAUSE_ONWARD_GRANT: Final[str] = "onward_grant"
    32	CAUSE_HUMAN_GRANT: Final[str] = "human_grant"
    33	RESOLVED_BY_AUTO: Final[str] = "auto"
    34	RESOLVED_BY_HUMAN: Final[str] = "human"
